Mice ran through corridors with different\nvisual textures and received water rewards in one corridor type.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport os\nimport sys\nimport time\nimport argparse\nimport pickle\nimport numpy as np\nfrom collections import defaultdict\nimport gc\nimport warnings\nwarnings.filterwarnings('ignore')\n\n# =============================================================================\n# Constants and configuration\n# =============================================================================\nDATA_ROOT = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)), 'data')\nCODE_ROOT = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)), 'code')\n\n# Brain region mapping from iarea values (from utils.py: neu_area_ID)\nAREA_MAP = {\n    8: 'V1',\n    0: 'mHV', 1: 'mHV', 2: 'mHV', 9: 'mHV',\n    5: 'lHV', 6: 'lHV',\n    3: 'aHV', 4: 'aHV',\n    -1: 'other', 7: 'other',\n}\n\nBRAIN_REGIONS = ['V1', 'mHV', 'lHV', 'aHV', 'other']\n\n# Position binning: 4 bins of 1m (10 dm) each over [0, 40) dm corridor\n# Gray space (40-60 dm) goes to last bin\nPOS_BIN_EDGES = [0, 10, 20, 30, 60.01]  # last bin includes gray space\nPOS_BIN_LABELS = ['0-1m', '1-2m', '2-3m', '3m+']\n\n# =============================================================================\n# Data loading functions (adapted from reference code utils.py)\n# =============================================================================\n\ndef load_spk(db):\n    \"\"\"Load neural data and concatenate planes. Returns (n_neurons, n_frames).\"\"\"\n    fn = '%s_%s_%s_neural_data.npy' % (db['mname'], db['datexp'], db['blk'])\n    spk_path = os.path.join(DATA_ROOT, 'spk', fn)\n    spk = np.concatenate(\n        [nspk for nspk in np.load(spk_path, allow_pickle=True).item()['spks']], 0\n    )\n    return spk\n\n\ndef load_retino(db):\n    \"\"\"Load retinotopy data. Returns iarea array.\"\"\"\n    fn = '%s_%s_trans.npz' % (db['mname'], db['datexp'])\n    dtrans = np.load(os.path.join(DATA_ROOT, 'retinotopy', fn), allow_pickle=True)\n    return dtrans['iarea']\n\n\ndef load_beh(exp_type):\n    \"\"\"Load behavior data for an experiment type.\"\"\"\n    fn = os.path.join(DATA_ROOT, 'beh', 'Beh_%s.npy' % exp_type)\n    return np.load(fn, allow_pickle=True).item()\n\n\ndef get_beh_key(db):\n    \"\"\"Get the behavior dictionary key for a session.\"\"\"\n    if 'stimtype' in db and db['stimtype']:\n        return '%s_%s_%s_%s' % (db['mname'], db['datexp'], db['blk'], db['stimtype'])\n    return '%s_%s_%s' % (db['mname'], db['datexp'], db['blk'])\n\n\n# =============================================================================\n# Build session list\n# =============================================================================\n\ndef build_session_list():\n    \"\"\"Build list of unique sessions with their metadata.\n\n    Returns list of dicts with keys: mname, datexp, blk, exp_type, db_entry, beh_key\n    \"\"\"\n    exp_info = np.load(\n        os.path.join(DATA_ROOT, 'beh', 'Imaging_Exp_info.npy'), allow_pickle=True\n    ).item()\n\n    seen = set()\n    sessions = []\n\n    for exp_type in exp_info:\n        for db in exp_info[exp_type]:\n            key = (db['mname'], db['datexp'], db['blk'])\n            if key in seen:\n                continue\n            seen.add(key)\n\n            beh_key = get_beh_key(db)\n            sessions.append({\n                'mname': db['mname'],\n                'datexp': db['datexp'],\n                'blk': db['blk'],\n                'exp_type': exp_type,\n                'db_entry': db,\n                'beh_key': beh_key,\n                'session_key': key,\n            })\n\n    # Sort by mouse name, then date\n    sessions.sort(key=lambda s: (s['mname'], s['datexp']))\n    return sessions\n\n\ndef compute_training_days(sessions):\n    \"\"\"Compute ordinal training day for each session within each mouse.\"\"\"\n    mouse_sessions = defaultdict(list)\n    for i, s in enumerate(sessions):\n        mouse_sessions[s['mname']].append((s['datexp'], i))\n\n    days = np.zeros(len(sessions), dtype=np.float32)\n    for mname, sess_list in mouse_sessions.items():\n        sess_list.sort(key=lambda x: x[0])  # sort by date\n        for day_idx, (datexp, global_idx) in enumerate(sess_list):\n            days[global_idx] = float(day_idx)\n\n    return days\n\n\n# =============================================================================\n# Processing functions\n# =============================================================================\n\ndef compute_speed_quartiles(sessions, beh_cache):\n    \"\"\"Compute global speed quartiles across all sessions.\"\"\"\n    print(\"Computing global speed quartiles...\")\n    all_speeds = []\n    for s in sessions:\n        beh = beh_cache[s['exp_type']][s['beh_key']]\n        speeds = beh['ft_RunSpeed']\n        all_speeds.append(speeds)\n\n    all_speeds = np.concatenate(all_speeds)\n    quartiles = np.percentile(all_speeds, [25, 50, 75])\n    print(f\"  Speed quartiles: {quartiles}\")\n    print(f\"  Speed range: [{all_speeds.min():.1f}, {all_speeds.max():.1f}]\")\n    return quartiles\n\n\ndef get_all_stimuli(sessions, beh_cache):\n    \"\"\"Get sorted list of all unique stimulus names.\"\"\"\n    all_stim = set()\n    for s in sessions:\n        beh = beh_cache[s['exp_type']][s['beh_key']]\n        for wn in beh['UniqWalls']:\n            all_stim.add(str(wn))\n    return sorted(all_stim)\n\n\ndef digitize_position(pos):\n    \"\"\"Discretize position into 4 bins of 1m (10dm) each.\n    Bins: [0,10), [10,20), [20,30), [30,60+)\n    \"\"\"\n    bins = np.digitize(pos, [10, 20, 30])\n    return bins.astype(np.int64)\n\n\ndef digitize_speed(speed, quartiles):\n    \"\"\"Discretize running speed into 4 quartile bins.\"\"\"\n    bins = np.digitize(speed, quartiles)\n    return bins.astype(np.int64)\n\n\ndef make_lick_vector(beh, start_fr, end_fr):\n    \"\"\"Create binary lick vector for frames [start_fr, end_fr).\n\n    LickFr values are fractional frame indices.\n    \"\"\"\n    n_frames = end_fr - start_fr\n    lick_vec = np.zeros(n_frames, dtype=np.int64)\n\n    lick_frs = beh['LickFr']\n    # Round to nearest integer frame\n    lick_frs_int = np.round(lick_frs).astype(int)\n    # Filter to frames within this trial\n    mask = (lick_frs_int >= start_fr) & (lick_frs_int < end_fr)\n    if mask.any():\n        trial_lick_frs = lick_frs_int[mask] - start_fr\n        # Clip to valid range\n        trial_lick_frs = np.clip(trial_lick_frs, 0, n_frames - 1)\n        lick_vec[trial_lick_frs] = 1\n\n    return lick_vec\n\n\ndef get_brain_region_idx(iarea):\n    \"\"\"Map iarea values to brain_region indices.\n\n    BRAIN_REGIONS = ['V1', 'mHV', 'lHV', 'aHV', 'other']\n    \"\"\"\n    region_to_idx = {r: i for i, r in enumerate(BRAIN_REGIONS)}\n    # Vectorized mapping\n    idx = np.full(len(iarea), region_to_idx['other'], dtype=np.int64)\n    for area_val, region_name in AREA_MAP.items():\n        mask = iarea == area_val\n        idx[mask] = region_to_idx[region_name]\n    return idx\n\n\ndef process_session(session, beh, training_day, speed_quartiles, stim_to_idx, frame_period):\n    \"\"\"Process a single session into decoder format.\n\n    Returns:\n        neural_trials: list of (n_neurons, n_timepoints) arrays\n        input_trials: list of (4, n_timepoints) arrays\n        output_trials: list of (4, n_timepoints) arrays\n        brain_region_idx: (n_neurons,) array\n    \"\"\"\n    t0 = time.time()\n\n    # Load neural data\n    spk = load_spk(session['db_entry'])\n    n_neurons, n_total_frames = spk.shape\n\n    # Load retinotopy\n    iarea = load_retino(session['db_entry'])\n    brain_reg_idx = get_brain_region_idx(iarea)\n\n    # Verify consistency\n    assert len(iarea) == n_neurons, \\\n        f\"Neuron count mismatch: spk={n_neurons}, retinotopy={len(iarea)}\"\n\n    ntrials = beh['ntrials']\n    StartFr = beh['StartFr'].astype(int)\n    EndFr = beh['EndFr'].astype(int)\n    SoundFr = beh['SoundFr']\n    WallName = beh['WallName']\n    isRew = beh['isRew']\n    ft_Pos = beh['ft_Pos']\n    ft_RunSpeed = beh['ft_RunSpeed']\n\n    neural_trials = []\n    input_trials = []\n    output_trials = []\n\n    skipped = 0\n    for t in range(ntrials):\n        start = StartFr[t]\n        end = EndFr[t]\n\n        # Skip if frames out of bounds\n        if start < 0 or end > n_total_frames or end <= start:\n            skipped += 1\n            continue\n\n        # Also clip end to available frames in beh arrays\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

started**: 2026-03-24\n- **Goal**: Convert to decoder-compatible format\n\n## Step 0: Setup and Initialization\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\nDirectory contents:\n- `code/` - Reference code: utils.py, fig1-5.py, S6.py, data_process_script.ipynb, Figures.ipynb\n- `data/beh/` - 26 behavioral .npy files + Imaging_Exp_info.npy\n- `data/spk/` - 89 neural data files (`{mouse}_{date}_{blk}_neural_data.npy`)\n- `data/retinotopy/` - 89 retinotopy files (`{mouse}_{date}_trans.npz`) + `areas.npz`\n- `data/process_data/` - empty (intermediate results would go here)\n- `paper.pdf`, `methods.txt`, `decoder.py`, `train_decoder.py`\n\nPython: numpy 2.3.5, torch 2.6.0+cu124, GPU available\n\n---\n\n## Step 1: Reference Code Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Key Functions Identified\n| Function | File | Stage | Purpose |\n|----------|------|-------|---------|\n| `load_spk(db)` | utils.py | LOADING | Load neural data, concatenate spks list into (n_neurons, n_frames) |\n| `load_retino(db)` | utils.py | LOADING | Load retinotopy (xy_t, iarea), create area index via `neu_area_ID` |\n| `load_exp_beh(root, exp_type)` | utils.py | LOADING | Load behavior file for an experiment type |\n| `neu_area_ID(iarea)` | utils.py | PROCESSING | Map iarea values to brain regions: V1(8), mHV(0,1,2,9), lHV(5,6), aHV(3,4) |\n| `get_interpPos_spk()` | utils.py | PROCESSING | Interpolate neural activity into (neurons, trials, 60 position bins) |\n| `spk_pos_interp()` | utils.py | PROCESSING | Core interpolation: raw spk + cumulative pos -> trial x position matrix |\n| `dprime()` | utils.py | PROCESSING | Compute d-prime for stimulus selectivity |\n| `lickCount()` | utils.py | PROCESSING | Binary lick response per trial (before/after reward) |\n| `get_cat_id()` | utils.py | PROCESSING | Get stimulus category IDs from wall names and reward info |\n| `Get_dprime_selective_neuron()` | utils.py | CURATION | Select stimulus-selective neurons using d-prime, filter by corridor activity |\n| `Get_coding_direction()` | utils.py | PROCESSING | Coding direction analysis with normalization and cross-validation |\n\n### Key Data Flow\n1. **Neural data**: Loaded via `load_spk()` -> concatenates multiple planes of Suite2p output -> shape (n_neurons, n_frames)\n2. **Behavior**: Loaded from `Beh_{exp_type}.npy` -> dictionary with per-session behavior data\n3. **Retinotopy**: Maps neurons to visual cortex areas (V1, mHV, lHV, aHV) via `iarea` values\n4. **Processing**: Reference code primarily uses position-interpolated data (60 bins per trial) for analyses. The raw data is at ~3.17 Hz frame rate.\n5. **Neuron filtering in reference**: Corridor-active neurons selected (higher activity in texture vs gray space). D-prime used for stimulus selectivity analysis. No explicit quality filtering of neurons.\n6. **DeltaF/F**: NOT needed - data is already Suite2p deconvolved traces (spike deconvolution with 0.75s decay timescale). All analyses use deconvolved traces.\n\n### Session Database Structure (Imaging_Exp_info.npy)\n- 23 experiment types, 142 session-experiment pairs total\n- 89 unique sessions across 19 mice\n- Same Time to sound cue (seconds): positive = time until cue, negative = time since cue\n        sound_fr = SoundFr[t]\n        frame_indices = np.arange(start, end, dtype=np.float64)\n        time_to_sound = (sound_fr - frame_indices) * frame_period  # positive before, negative after\n\n        # 2. Day of training (constant for trial)\n        day = np.full(n_tp, training_day, dtype=np.float32)\n\n        # 3. Time since trial start (seconds)\n        time_since_start = (frame_indices - start) * frame_period\n\n        # 4. Reward availability\n        rew = np.full(n_tp, float(isRew[t]), dtype=np.float32)\n\n        inp = np.stack([time_to_sound, day, time_since_start, rew], axis=0).astype(np.float32)\n\n        # --- Outputs ---\n        # 1. Visual stimulus category (per-trial, broadcast)\n        stim_name = str(WallName[t])\n        stim_idx = stim_to_idx[stim_name]\n        stim_out = np.full(n_tp, stim_idx, dtype=np.int64)\n\n        # 2. Licking (binary, time-varying)\n        lick = make_lick_vector(beh, start, end)\n\n        # 3. Position (4 bins, time-varying)\n        pos = ft_Pos[start:end]\n        pos_bin = digitize_position(pos)\n\n        # 4. Running speed (4 bins, time-varying)\n        speed = ft_RunSpeed[start:end]\n        speed_bin = digitize_speed(speed, speed_quartiles)\n\n        out = np.stack([stim_out, lick, pos_bin, speed_bin], axis=0).astype(np.int64)\n\n        neural_trials.append(neural)\n        input_trials.append(inp)\n        output_trials.append(out)\n\n    elapsed = time.time() - t0\n    if skipped > 0:\n        print(f\"    Skipped {skipped}/{ntrials} trials\")\n\n    return neural_trials, input_trials, output_trials, brain_reg_idx, elapsed\n\n\n# =============================================================================\n# Visualization\n# =============================================================================\n\ndef plot_processing(session, beh, neural_trials, input_trials, output_trials,\n                    stim_names, speed_quartiles, session_idx):\n    \"\"\"Plot processing steps for visual verification.\"\"\"\n    import matplotlib\n    matplotlib.use('Agg')\n    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt\n\n    n_trials_plot = min(5, len(neural_trials))\n    fig, axes = plt.subplots(6, n_trials_plot, figsize=(4 * n_trials_plot, 18))\n    if n_trials_plot == 1:\n        axes = axes[:, np.newaxis]\n\n    for t in range(n_trials_plot):\n        neural = neural_trials[t]\n        inp = input_trials[t]\n        out = output_trials[t]\n        n_tp = neural.shape[1]\n        time_axis = np.arange(n_tp)\n\n        # 1. datenum to seconds\n    frame_period = float(np.nanmedian(dt))\n    print(f\"  Frame period: {frame_period:.4f} s ({1/frame_period:.2f} Hz)\")\n\n    # Process sessions\n    print(f\"\\nProcessing {len(sessions)} sessions...\")\n    neural_all = []\n    input_all = []\n    output_all = []\n    subjects_list = []\n    subject_idx_list = []\n    brain_region_idx_all = []\n\n    subjects = sorted(set(s['mname'] for s in sessions))\n    subject_to_idx = {s: i for i, s in enumerate(subjects)}\n\n    for i, session in enumerate(sessions):\n        sid = f\"{session['mname']}_{session['datexp']}_{session['blk']}\"\n        print(f\"  [{i+1}/{len(sessions)}] {sid} ({session['exp_type']})...\")\n\n        beh = beh_cache[session['exp_type']][session['beh_key']]\n        day = training_days[i]\n\n        neural_trials, input_trials, output_trials, brain_reg_idx, elapsed = \\\n            process_session(session, beh, day, speed_quartiles, stim_to_idx, frame_period)\n\n        if len(neural_trials) < 2:\n            print(f\"    WARNING: Only {len(neural_trials)} trials, skipping session\")\n            continue\n\n        neural_all.append(neural_trials)\n        input_all.append(input_trials)\n        output_all.append(output_trials)\n        subject_idx_list.append(subject_to_idx[session['mname']])\n        brain_region_idx_all.append(brain_reg_idx)\n\n        n_neurons = neural_trials[0].shape[0]\n        n_trials = len(neural_trials)\n        mean_tp = np.mean([t.shape[1] for t in neural_trials])\n        neural_mb = sum(t.nbytes for t in neural_trials) / 1e6\n        print(f\"    {n_trials} trials, {n_neurons} neurons, mean {mean_tp:.0f} frames/trial, {neural_mb:.0f}MB, {elapsed:.1f}s\")\n\n        if args.show_processing and i < 2:\n            plot_processing(session, beh, neural_trials, input_trials, output_trials,\n                          all_stimuli, speed_quartiles, i)\n\n        # Force garbage collection periodically\n        if (i + 1) % 10 == 0:\n            gc.collect()\n            total_neural_mb = sum(sum(t.nbytes for t in s) for s in neural_all) / 1e6\n            print(f\"    [Memory] Total neural so far: {total_neural_mb/1000:.1f} GB\")\n\n    # This matches the reference usage (e.g., `spk[SoundFr[n] - ranges[0] : SoundFr[n] + ranges[1]]` in `spk_2_cue`, line 943).\n\nOne subtlety: convert_data.py never clips or validates that `sound_fr` falls within `[start, end]`. If `sound_fr` is outside the trial window (which can happen legitimately — the cue might be in a different trial), `time_to_sound` values will all be either all-positive or all-negative across the trial. This is not necessarily wrong (it preserves the signed distance), but it is not validated. The reference code uses `SoundFr` to index directly into the spike array without checking bounds either, so this is consistent behavior.\n\n---\n\n### 5. Frame Period / Timing\n\n**Reference (`utils.py`):** There is **no `frame_period` or `ft` timestamp calculation anywhere** in utils.py. The reference code works entirely in frame-index space (e.g., `spk[:, SoundFr[n] - 15 : SoundFr[n] + 15]`) and never converts to seconds. The notebook data dictionary confirms `beh['ft']` exists (line 95: \"time stamp for each neural frame\"), but utils.py never uses it for timing.\n\n**`convert_data.py` (lines 451-456):**\n```python\nsample_beh = beh_cache[sessions[0]['exp_type']][sessions[0]['beh_key']]\nft = sample_beh['ft']\ndt = np.diff(ft) * 24 * 3600  # datenum to seconds\nframe_period = float(np.nanmedian(dt))\n```\n\n**Verdict: Cannot confirm against reference — the reference code has no frame period computation.** The approach in convert_data.py is reasonable and uses the standard MATLAB datenum-to-seconds conversion (`* 24 * 3600`), and uses the median across frame intervals robustly. However, there are two concerns:\n\n- **Single-session sampling**: `frame_period` is computed from only `sessions[0]` and used for **all sessions**. If frame rates differ across sessions (different days, different microscope settings), this could introduce timing errors. The reference code avoids this entirely by never needing seconds. Consider computing a per-session `frame_period` from each session's own `beh['ft']`.\n\n- **No validation**: There is no check that `ft` is actually a MATLAB datenum (not already in seconds or some other unit). If any session uses a different time representation, the conversion will be silently wrong.\n\n---\n\n### 6. Trial Filtering — What Does the Reference Filter?\n\n**Reference (`utils.py`):** The reference code never works with raw `StartFr`/`EndFr` trial windows at all. Its primary data pathway is:\n\n1. Load full spike array (neurons × total_frames)\n2. Compute a `VRmove = beh['ft_move'] > 0` mask — **only frames where the VR is actually moving**\n3. Compute a `corr_fr = beh['ft_CorrSpc'] & VRmove` mask — **only frames inside the texture corridor AND moving**\n4. Optionally `grey_fr = beh['ft_GraySpc'] & VRmove`\n5. For analysis, use only frames passing these masks\n\nThis means the reference code **implicitly filters out all stationary frames** (`ft_move == 0`) and **all gray-space frames** from its neural analyses. It also filters out inter-trial frames via `ft_trInd` when working trial-by-trial.\n\n**`convert_data.py`:**\n- Does apply bounds-checking (`start < 0`, `end > n_total_frames`, `end <= start`, `n_tp < 2`) — reasonable safety checks.\n- **Does NOT apply `VRmove` filtering.** All frames within `[StartFr, EndFr)` are included, even stationary frames where `beh['ft_move'] == 0`. In the reference code those frames are excluded from every analysis.\n- **Does NOT apply `ft_CorrSpc` filtering.** Gray-space frames (the last ~20 dm of each trial) are included in convert_data.py's trials. In the reference code, these are separately tracked but excluded from texture-selectivity analyses.\n- Does not filter the first 200 trials per session (the reference's `get_lick_response_in_zone` does `idx[200:] = False`, but this is specific to one behavioral analysis, not a general filter).\n\n**This is the most significant discrepancy.** The reference code's neural analyses consistently exclude stationary frames (`ft_move == 0`) from all comparisons. Convert_data.py includes them, which means the neural data array for each trial contains a mix of active-running frames and stationary frames. Depending on what the downstream decoder does, this could dilute or confound signals that the reference paper found using only running frames.\n\n---\n\n## Summary of Discrepancies\n\n| # | Item | Status | Severity |\n|---|------|--------|----------|\n| 1 | `load_spk` concatenation | Matches exactly | None |\n| 2 | AREA_MAP numeric values | Matches for all 4 named regions; `other` category is an addition | Minor (design choice, not a bug) |\n| 3 | Position range `[0, 60]` dm | Correct | None |\n| 4 | `SoundFr` as per-trial scalar | Correctly used; bounds not validated | Minor |\n| 5 | Frame period computation | No reference equivalent; computed from single session only | Moderate — could be inaccurate across sessions |\n| 6 | Trial filtering / `VRmove` | **Critical gap**: convert_data.py does NOT filter stationary frames (`ft_move == 0`), which the reference code consistently applies to all analyses | **High** |\n\n**The single most important discrepancy is #6**: convert_data.py includes all frames in `[StartFr, EndFr)` regardless of whether the mouse is actually running. The reference code universally filters to `VRmove = beh['ft_move'][:nfr] > 0` before any analysis. Gray-space frames (position 40–60 dm) are also worth noting — they are included in convert_data.py's position output but are excluded from the reference's texture-response analyses. Brain Region Mapping (`AREA_MAP` vs `neu_area_ID()`)\n\n**Reference (`utils.py`, lines 312-324):**\n```python\ndef neu_area_ID(iarea):\n    area_name = ['V1', 'mHV', 'lHV', 'aHV']\n    idx = {}\n    if ar=='V1':    idx[ar] = iarea==8\n    elif ar=='mHV': idx[ar] = (iarea==0) | (iarea==1) | (iarea==2) | (iarea==9)\n    elif ar=='lHV': idx[ar] = (iarea==5) | (iarea==6)\n    elif ar=='aHV': idx[ar] = (iarea==3) | (iarea==4)\n    return idx   # note: returns 4 named regions only, no 'other'\n```\n\n**`convert_data.py` (`AREA_MAP`, lines 31-37):**\n```python\nAREA_MAP = {\n    8: 'V1',\n    0: 'mHV', 1: 'mHV', 2: 'mHV', 9: 'mHV',\n    5: 'lHV', 6: 'lHV',\n    3: 'aHV', 4: 'aHV',\n    -1: 'other', 7: 'other',\n}\n```\n\n**Verdict: MATCHES for the four named regions.** The numeric `iarea` values for V1, mHV, lHV, and aHV are identical.\n\nHowever, there is a **notable difference in scope**: `neu_area_ID()` only defines the four named areas and produces no `other` category. Neurons with `iarea == -1` or `iarea == 7` are implicitly \"not in any named area.\" `convert_data.py` adds an explicit `'other'` category for those two values and maps all remaining unmapped iarea values to `'other'` by default. The reference code at multiple places uses `(arid!=-1) & (arid != 7)` as an exclusion filter (e.g., `Get_density_map`, line 408) rather than grouping them. This is a **design difference, not a bug**: convert_data.py's approach is more conservative and inclusive. Just be aware that neurons classified as `'other'` in convert_data.py are actively excluded (dropped) in the reference code's analyses.\n\n---\n\n### 3. The comment on line 41 says \"4 bins of 1m (10 dm) each over [0, 40) dm corridor\" — this is accurate for the texture area. 3]\nSoundDelPos: (485,), range: [30.4, 29072.1]\nrun_pos shape: (485, 60)\nft_WallID shape/dtype: (22476,), <U30\n[metadata] {\"noOutputExpected\": false}\n\n===== STEP 30 agent OBS =====\n1→Similar to previous work, we designed a visual discrimination task in head-fixed mice running through linear virtual reality corridors (Fig. 1a). Mice had to discriminate between visual texture patterns in two corridors; these corridors were repeated in pseudo-random order. The visual patterns in each corridor were obtained as ‘frozen’ crops from large photographs of naturalistic textures. For simplicity, we denote the stimuli as ‘leaf’ and ‘circle’, even though other visual stimuli were also used in some mice (‘rock’ and ‘bricks’; see Methods and Extended Data Fig. 1a). The visual stimuli were spatial frequency matched between categories to encourage the use of higher-order visual features in discrimination. A sound cue was presented at a random position inside each corridor and was followed by the availability of water in rewarded trials only (Fig. 1a). After approximately 2 weeks of training (Fig. 1b), mice demonstrated selective licking in the rewarded corridor in anticipation of reward delivery (Fig. 1c,d; error bars on all figures represent s.e.m.). After learning, we introduced unrewarded test stimuli ‘leaf2’ and ‘circle2’, which were different frozen crops of the same photographs. We then continued training with unrewarded leaf2 until the mice stopped licking to this stimulus, at which point we introduced another test stimulus (‘leaf3’) as well as spatially shuffled versions of leaf1 (Fig. 1b).\n     2→\n     3→Mice in the unsupervised cohort also ran through the same corridors for similar periods of time, but did not receive water rewards and were not water restricted. We also studied a cohort of mice that ran through a virtual reality corridor with gratings on the walls. These served as a control to show the effect of simple exposure to virtual reality, independent of the image patterns shown. To ensure that all mice had a comparable visual experience, we fixed the speed of the virtual reality when mice ran faster than a speed threshold, and kept the virtual reality stationary otherwise. The overall running speeds were similar before and after learning, and between the task and unsupervised cohorts (Extended Data Fig. 2). We only considered timepoints during running for analysis, which removed time periods when the task mice stopped to collect water rewards.\n     4→\n     5→Before and after learning, we recorded from large neural populations across many visual areas simultaneously using a two-photon mesoscope (Fig. 1e). We ran Suite2p on this data to obtain the activity traces from 20,547 to 89,577 neurons in each recording.\n     6→\n     7→It is possible that the neural plasticity that we observed was due to spatial learning and navigation signals, which have been found to modulate firing rates even in the visual cortex. Alternatively, the neural plasticity might be due to adaptation to the visual statistics of the natural images that we presented. To distinguish between a spatial and a visual plasticity hypothesis, we next introduced two unrewarded test stimuli, leaf2 and circle2, which contained similar visual features to leaf1 and circle1, respectively, but were arranged in different spatial configurations (Fig. 2a). \n     8→\n     9→Methods\n    10→\n    11→We performed 89 recordings in 19 mice bred to express GCaMP6s in excitatory neurons: TetO-GCaMP6s × camK2a-tTa mice.\n    12→\n    13→The mice were free to run on an air-floating ball.\n    14→\n    15→Visual stimuli\n    16→\n    17→We showed virtual reality corridors to the mice on three perpendicular LED tablet screens, which surrounded each mouse (covering 270° of their visual field of view). To present the stimuli, we used PsychToolbox-3 in MATLAB. The virtual reality corridors were each 4 m long, with 2 m of grey space between corridors. The corridors were shown in a random order. The mice moved forward in the virtual reality corridors by running faster than a threshold of 6 cm s−1, but the virtual corridors always moved at a constant speed (60 cm s−1) as long as mice kept running faster than the threshold. Running was detected using an optical tracking sensor placed close to the ball.\n    18→\n    19→The virtual reality corridors were created by concatenating four random crops from one of four large texture images: circle, leaf, rock and brick (Extended Data Fig. 1a). Grating stimuli (with angles of 0° and 45°) were also used to create the virtual reality corridors for a subset of mice (‘unsupervised gratings’ and VRg mice).\n    20→\n    21→For behaviour-only experiments, each mouse was trained with rewards on one random pair of stimuli, such as leaf–circle or rock–brick. Mice with unsupervised pretraining (VRn mice) were pretrained on the same pair on which they were trained with rewards.\n    22→\n    23→For imaging experiments, mice from both the task and the unsupervised cohorts were only trained on or exposed to one pair of stimuli, such as leaf–circle or rock–brick. For the mice exposed to the grating stimuli, we presented more than one pair of naturalistic stimuli before and after exposure to the gratings stimuli and recorded the neural responses to the naturalistic stimuli pairs, so each mouse can have more than one imaging session before and after. We also presented more than one pair of naturalistic stimuli to the naive mice, so each naive mouse can have more than one imaging session for testing naive responses.\n    24→\n    25→The two different types of leaf1-swap stimuli (Extended Data Figs. 6f and 7a) were introduced separately for task mice but are pooled together for statistics analysis. The two leaf1-swap stimuli were introduced in the same session for unsupervised and naive mice, but were treated as two different data points and pooled together for statistical analyses.\n    26→\n    27→Behavioural training\n    28→\n    29→All animals were handled via refined handling techniques for at least 3 days before being acclimated to head fixation on the ball. Animals were acclimated gradually (0.5–1 h per day) on the ball \n\n===== STEP 40 agent OBS =====\nTrial 0: StartFr=5, GrayFr=30, EndFr=42, dur=37 frames\n  Position range: [0.0, 57.5]\n  Corridor Here is the complete analysis across all six questions.\n\n---\n\n## Comparison Report: `convert_data.py` vs `utils.py`\n\n---\n\n### 1. `load_spk()` - Concatenation of `spks` Planes\n\n**Reference (`utils.py`, line 338-342):**\n```python\ndef load_spk(db, root=''):\n    fn = '%s_%s_%s_neural_data.npy'%(db['mname'],db['datexp'],db['blk'])\n    spk_path = os.path.join(root, fn)\n    spk = np.concatenate([nspk for nspk in np.load(spk_path, allow_pickle=True).item()['spks']],0)\n    return spk\n```\n\n**`convert_data.py` (`load_spk`, lines 50-57):**\n```python\nspk = np.concatenate(\n    [nspk for nspk in np.load(spk_path, allow_pickle=True).item()['spks']], 0\n)\n```\n\n**Verdict: MATCHES.** The logic — iterate over `['spks']` planes, concatenate along axis 0 — is identical. The only difference is path construction (convert_data.py hardcodes `DATA_ROOT/spk/` while utils.py takes a `root` argument), which is intentional and functionally equivalent given how convert_data.py is set up.\n\n---\n\n### 2. Brain Region Mapping (`AREA_MAP` vs `neu_area_ID()`)\n\n**Reference (`utils.py`, lines 312-324):**\n```python\ndef neu_area_ID(iarea):\n    area_name = ['V1', 'mHV', 'lHV', 'aHV']\n    idx = {}\n    if ar=='V1':    idx[ar] = iarea==8\n    elif ar=='mHV': idx[ar] = (iarea==0) | (iarea==1) | (iarea==2) | (iarea==9)\n    elif ar=='lHV': idx[ar] = (iarea==5) | (iarea==6)\n    elif ar=='aHV': idx[ar] = (iarea==3) | (iarea==4)\n    return idx   # note: returns 4 named regions only, no 'other'\n```\n\n**`convert_data.py` (`AREA_MAP`, lines 31-37):**\n```python\nAREA_MAP = {\n    8: 'V1',\n    0: 'mHV', 1: 'mHV', 2: 'mHV', 9: 'mHV',\n    5: 'lHV', 6: 'lHV',\n    3: 'aHV', 4: 'aHV',\n    -1: 'other', 7: 'other',\n}\n```\n\n**Verdict: MATCHES for the four named regions.** The numeric `iarea` values for V1, mHV, lHV, and aHV are identical.\n\nHowever, there is a **notable difference in scope**: `neu_area_ID()` only defines the four named areas and produces no `other` category. Neurons with `iarea == -1` or `iarea == 7` are implicitly \"not in any named area.\" `convert_data.py` adds an explicit `'other'` category for those two values and maps all remaining unmapped iarea values to `'other'` by default. The reference code at multiple places uses `(arid!=-1) & (arid != 7)` as an exclusion filter (e.g., `Get_density_map`, line 408) rather than grouping them. This is a **design difference, not a bug**: convert_data.py's approach is more conservative and inclusive. Just be aware that neurons classified as `'other'` in convert_data.py are actively excluded (dropped) in the reference code's analyses.\n\n---\n\n### 3. Position Range `[0, 60]` dm\n\n**Reference code:**\nThe corridor is consistently described and plotted as `[0, 60]` dm = 6 meters throughout the codebase. From the notebook data_process_script.ipynb (line 193):\n> \"bins of position: 60, each bin is 1 decimeter, total length of corridor is 6 meters\"\n\nThe interpolation function `get_interpPos_spk` uses `n_bins=60, lengths=CL` where `CL = beh['Corridor_Length']`. All figure axes show `xtick=[[0, 20, 40, 60], [0, 2, 4, 6]]`, confirming the full corridor is 60 dm (6 m): 40 dm texture area + 20 dm gray space.\n\n**`convert_data.py`** (`POS_BIN_EDGES`, line 43):\n```python\nPOS_BIN_EDGES = [0, 10, 20, 30, 60.01]  # last bin includes gray space\n```\nAnd `digitize_position` bins using `[10, 20, 30]` as edges.\n\n**Verdict: Consistent.** The maximum of 60 dm is correct. The comment on line 41 says \"4 bins of 1m (10 dm) each over [0, 40) dm corridor\" — this is accurate for the texture area. The last bin `[30, 60+)` correctly lumps bins 30–40 dm (end of texture) and 40–60 dm (gray space) into one bin. The 60 dm ceiling matches the reference exactly.\n\n---\n\n### 4. `SoundFr` - Per-Trial Scalar or Something Else?\n\n**Reference (`utils.py`):**\n\n`SoundFr` is used in two distinct ways:\n\n**a) As a per-trial array of frame indices** (length = `ntrials`):\n- `beh['SoundFr'].astype(int)` — lines 831, 893, 939\n- Used as: `spk[:, SoundFr[n]]` (indexing a single frame per trial, line 943), `spk[:, SoundFr[stim1]]` (boolean index of per-trial frames, line 860/902)\n- The notebook data dictionary confirms: `\"beh['SoundFr']: neural frame when sound cue was delivered\"` — one frame index per trial\n\n**b) As global frame indices into the spike array**, using trial boolean masks to pick which trials' cue frames to use — meaning `SoundFr` is a 1D array of shape `(ntrials,)` where each element is an absolute frame index in the spike recording.\n\n**`convert_data.py` (line 277-279):**\n```python\nsound_fr = SoundFr[t]   # scalar: the cue frame index for trial t\nframe_indices = np.arange(start, end, dtype=np.float64)\ntime_to_sound = (sound_fr - frame_indices) * frame_period\n```\n\n**Verdict: CORRECT.** `SoundFr` is a per-trial array; `SoundFr[t]` is the absolute frame index of the sound cue for trial `t`. convert_data.py correctly indexes it per-trial and uses it as a scalar reference point to compute relative time within the trial. This matches the reference usage (e.g., `spk[SoundFr[n] - ranges[0] : SoundFr[n] + ranges[1]]` in `spk_2_cue`, line 943).\n\nOne subtlety: convert_data.py never clips or validates that `sound_fr` falls within `[start, end]`. If `sound_fr` is outside the trial window (which can happen legitimately — the cue might be in a different trial), `time_to_sound` values will all be either all-positive or all-negative across the trial. This is not necessarily wrong (it preserves the signed distance), but it is not validated. The reference code uses `SoundFr` to index directly into the spike array without checking bounds either, so this is consistent behavior.\n\n---\n\n### 5. Frame Period / Timing\n\n**Reference (`utils.py`):** There is **no `frame_period` or `ft` timestamp calculation anywhere** in utils.py. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Licking*?\n\ni. The code rounds fractional lick frame indices to the nearest integer, keeps only licks whose integer frame lies within the current trial window, shifts them into trial-relative coordinates, clips to valid indices, and writes 1s into a binary vector.\n\nii. ```python\ndef make_lick_vector(beh, start_fr, end_fr):\n    n_frames = end_fr - start_fr\n    lick_vec = np.zeros(n_frames, dtype=np.int64)\n\n    lick_frs = beh['LickFr']\n    lick_frs_int = np.round(lick_frs).astype(int)\n    mask = (lick_frs_int >= start_fr) & (lick_frs_int < end_fr)\n    if mask.any():\n        trial_lick_frs = lick_frs_int[mask] - start_fr\n        trial_lick_frs = np.clip(trial_lick_frs, 0, n_frames - 1)\n        lick_vec[trial_lick_frs] = 1\n```\n\niii.
